- **Cold deep net (`EnsureDeepNet`, `BuildLatticeNeighbours`).** The neighbour table reads a flat grid over the floor's bounding box (`FTransitFlatIndex`, a reused buffer; the hash stays as the fallback when the box is over 64 x the cell count + 4,096), the four orthogonals found once and the diagonals read from them. One heap and one grid buffer are reused across all 80 fields (`RunLatticeField`; `BuildLatticeField` with a table now calls it too). The surface-leg memo (`SurfaceRoutes`) is kept across a rebuild while `RoadGraph.BuiltVersion` is the one it was planned under (`BuiltVersion` only ever rises, a load resets the net, and the memo is a pure function of the built graph). **Not done:** per-floor caching of fields or tables (not needed; every budget passes without it).
- **`PlanDeepRoute`.** A binary heap keyed (distance, node index) with lazy deletion (`FTransitSearch`: raw arrays, a hand-written sift; the pop order is the strict total order alone, and every pair is pushed once, so it pops exactly the sequence the old linear scan did). The edge prices are stored in the net (`FRHDeepNet::Edges`, parallel to the old `Adj`, which is gone: kind, floors or lattice cm or head cost) and the robot's wait is read once a shaft a call (`RideShaftUnits[S] + RideFloorUnits x floors`, the same integer as the old `RideUnits`); each node's edges are priced once, when it settles; the start relaxes only the heads, the portals on its level, or its column; the end is priced from the popped portal alone. Portals are indexed by level (`LevelPortals`, filtered on column or dig: `FindPortalColumnAt` scans one short list); inline capacity 512 nodes (the worst case is 498) and 256 heap items, a 32-slot chain, the three path arrays reserved to 64. Every tie-break, the strict-improvement relaxation and the iteration order are unchanged.
- **Flows (`DeriveCommuteFlows`).** The shaft of a floor-to-surface trip is picked once and passed to `GetCommuteDistanceVia` (the public `GetCommuteDistanceCm` calls it with no known shaft); `LongIds` is sorted only when it is out of order (it never is in a real colony); `Ids`, `Dists`, `LongIds`, `Trips`, `TripIndex` and the two transit trip arrays are reserved (the trip count from the last derive, 64 at least).
- **`-RHVerifyCaches` covers the one new cache.** A `SurfaceRoute` memo hit re-plans the leg and compares it (`RHVERIFY: the deep net's surface leg ... is stale`).

**Before and after (`-scale`, wall clock, two runs each).**

| budget | before | after | limit |
|---|---|---|---|
| cold deep net, 84 portals | 5.600, 5.579 ms | 2.299, 2.433, 2.329, 2.309 ms | 3 |
| 2,500 `PlanDeepRoute` | 25.964, 25.749 ms | 5.278, 5.296, 5.265, 5.270 ms | 10 |
| flows only, 2,000 deep residents | 1.025, 1.026 ms | 0.616, 0.617, 0.617, 0.615 ms | 1 |
| 1,000 cold reserve reads (20 floors) | 7.96, 7.80 ms | 1.62, 1.65 ms | 10 |
| 5-floor deep net / 2,500 `PlanDeepRoute` / flows | 1.88 / 8.20 / 0.328 ms | 0.89 / 4.78 / 0.202 ms | 3 / 10 / 1 |

`-scale`: **174 ok, 0 failures** (171 ok and 3 failures before). **No budget is still missed.** The tightest is the cold deep net at about 2.3 ms against 3 (about 0.94 ms of it is the cold dig labelling inside `EnsureDigInfo`, untouched). If a loaded machine ever tips it, per-floor caching of fields and tables is the next step (keyed on `Cells.Num()` plus a load counter, with its own `-RHVerifyCaches` check).

**Exactness evidence.**
- **A temporary probe** (removed; the tree has no `PROBE` string) took a CRC before the change and after it, on the final source, across three runs: every `PlanDeepRoute` result (valid flag, every waypoint, leg edge and level, `Next`, the start, and all eight cost fields), the deep net (portals, landings, digs, every field's distances and `Toward`, heads, head-to-head, the edges' targets, the fitted digs), and the flows (matrices, Ids, Dists, LongIds, shaft people, every trip). The route sets were 100 robots below plus a robot on every fitted column of every shaft and floor, each to every one of those and to two surface points, and from two surface points: 14,884 routes at five floors, 33,124 at 20 floors, the 20-floor set again with waits live (waits 0/0/30/75 s, read at a stamp past the lag and inside it). **Identical before and after, in all nine values:** five-floor net `194bb3de`, routes `c030a1f1`; 20-floor flows `c4017745`, net `210717b9`, routes `364772a8`, waits-new routes `d90ddaef`, waits-prev routes `de6f7467`, flows after a roll `df43e482`. The same run with the flat grid forced off (always the hash) gave the same values, so the fallback is exact too.
- **The kept surface-leg memo is not exercised by any suite** (the transit suite's one rebuild kept 0 legs; no `-RHVerifyCaches` run hit it). A temporary `-scale` probe (also removed) laid two roads between the heads, planned 1,200 routes, added cells to -1 (a rebuild at the same built-road version) and planned again under `-RHVerifyCaches`: 1,216 legs kept, 25,551 verified memo hits, 0 `RHVERIFY` errors.
- **Every `-scale` line outside the three budget lines is identical to before**, all 26,142 normalised lines (wall-clock figures masked): digests N=5000 `5f9d7352` (and 250, 1,000, 3,000), commute `5c609384` and `1e609e47`, rover plans `a579cc22`, approach `c754e9f0`, and the transit line's `48000 waypoints` and `cost sum 297968840000` (verified on a fresh run before the change, not taken from the notes). The only other line that differs is the "city grew the seed ... in N s of wall clock" figure.

**Battery.** `bash scripts/sim/rh_battery.sh 10`, run twice (the second after the temporary probe code was removed and the tree rebuilt): both `BATTERY COMPLETE: 0 suite(s) not green`, 51 logs, all newer than both dylibs, nothing in Source or docs/data newer than `baseline.log`; both pins hold (`power: gen 50 W load 20 W`, `deposit Regolith_A: 40645 kg`). `python3 scripts/sim/diff_battery.py Saved/baselines/battery_post_d6a Saved/RHBattery`: **zero differing lines except wall-clock digits**, named: `-traffic` X16 "119990 agent steps in 2.7 s" to 2.1 s (both runs; the ledgers on that line are identical), and in the second run `-eraparity` EP4 "119990 substeps in 0.6 s" to 0.7 s. No save bump.

**`-RHVerifyCaches`.** `transit` (190 checks), `tunnels` (152), `shafts` (145), `caverns` (156), `traffic` (99), `depth` (109), `deep` (94) and `districts` (118), each run on the final binary into a scratch directory (not `Saved/RHBattery`): 0 errors, 0 `RHVERIFY` lines, each completed.

**Playtest.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 perf_full guide full` is byte-identical to `Saved/RHPlaytest/d6a_full.txt` (1,468 lines, `cmp` clean, 0 sim errors).

**Notes.** The planner reads every shaft's wait once a call (at most 16 reads) instead of once per ride edge it prices. `RideShaftUnits` is indexed by a portal's shaft Id, which is its index in `Shafts` (`Fresh.Id = Shafts.Num()` at both places a shaft is added). The dig labelling inside a cold net build is still cold work and the largest single share of what is left.

### D6b floor shapes: districts as shapes on a floor, home and seat cells, growth inside shapes: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-22, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-22/d6-caverns-design.md`, section 2 of the MERGED DESIGN AND REVISION (RC 3, 4, 5, 6 and 10 apply), in the order of authority the director set: the DIRECTOR ANSWERs first. **Q-A (a):** reach, air, the Livable rating and the size minimum stay floor-wide (a Homes shape in one dig still breathes the floor's air: the City panel does not suggest otherwise). **Q-B (a) and Q-C (a)** touch D6a and D6d, not this gate. D6b adds no `docs/data` CSV row and no DataTable, so there is nothing to sync; its limits are code constants. D6a's `CarveLatticePoint` and horizontal eligibility rule are reused; no second carve path exists.

**What D6b builds.**
- **`FRHDistrict::Deep`** (`TArray<FIntVector>`, points (X, Y, Level) in region 0's underground frame, sorted by Level descending, then X, then Y): a district is exactly one of surface (`Lattice`), whole floor (both empty) or deep. `IsFloor()` is now `Level < 0 && Deep.IsEmpty()`, `IsDeep()` is new, and every existing caller keeps its meaning (the ground cache, the counts, the paint path, the load, the growth dispatch, both console lists and the district visualizer were each checked). `Lattice` stays surface-only.
- **`PaintDistrict` with `Value n >= 1`** (Level < 0) paints a rectangle on the floors `Level .. Level - n + 1`, snapped by `CmToLattice(shaft head, .)`. `Value` 0, NaN, below 1 or negative is today's whole-floor order line for line, and a surface order ignores it. Refusals, each with its reason: more than 8 floors (`RH::CavernMaxFloorsCap`), below `-MaxDepth`, a floor no shaft reaches, a side over 32, a floor held whole (naming the district to erase), a rectangle over two districts, another Kind over a shape, nothing but shaft columns, nothing new, and the total over `RH::MaxDeepPoints` (65,536). A floor is either whole or shaped: a whole-floor order on a floor that holds a shape is refused too. Same Kind joins (the district keeps its Id; its Level follows its shallowest floor); shaft columns are left out of the stored points; rock may be painted.
- **The ground cache** gains `DeepOwner` ((X, Y, Level) to Id) and `DeepFloor` (a mapped floor to the lowest shape Id on it); `-RHVerifyCaches` compares both. A floor is *mapped* when a deep district holds a point on it.
- **Membership, counts, seats.** A floor building belongs to the whole-floor owner, else the shape holding its head-frame lattice point; a resident of a mapped floor to the shape holding their **home cell**; a cell seat to the shape holding the cell (a module seat through its building, which is the same lattice point). A shape's Cells are its carved points, its Beds the beds of those cells on a rated floor (`CountFloorBeds`' rule per cell).
- **Home cells (RC 4)**, derived, never saved, for residents of mapped floors only: a resident with a bed piece lives in that piece's cell (read from `ColonistBedPiece`, never re-derived); the others, in Id order, take the legacy slots of the cells with no bed pieces in ordinal order, the k-th such cell offering `floor((k+1) x CPC) - floor(k x CPC)` of them, so the floor's total is `CountFloorBeds`' at any CPC; none left is `INDEX_NONE` (an overfull floor: they belong to no shape).
- **The keys (RC 6).** `FRHDistrictMembershipKey` and `FRHCommuteKey` gain `DeepCells` (`FloorCellsVersion`) and `DeepBeds` (`BedAssignVersion`), both 0 for ever while no shape exists, so the C5 counts and the X13 and X14 rebuild counts cannot move. With a shape a carve re-derives the legacy fill with no other version moved.
- **Growth inside a shape.** `RunGrowthStartCheck` tests `IsDeep()` first. **Homes: `ERHGrowthKind::DeepCells` (3)**, rated floors only: the shallowest floor of the shape that has something to offer, up to `GrowthCellsPerProject` points of it, the shape's free carved cells first (ascending ordinal), then eligible rock by (X, Y), a later pick leaning on an earlier one, never a point of an open tunnel's line. Bill: the bunks per point plus `O2FillKgPerCell` for each point to carve; `TotalSols` is `GrowthCellSols` times the floor's dig permille when something is carved, else plain. **Civic:** `PlanCivic`'s floor branch restricted to the shape (a free cell of it, else an eligible shape point carved, never the floor's next spiral cell). `FRHGrowthProject::Points` holds lattice points on the project's floor. Completion re-checks reach and rating, zones free cells, carves rock through `CarveLatticePoint` (which takes the point out of `Plan`), zones Living and furnishes as `CompleteFloorCells` does; a point that fails is refunded (its bunks, and its oxygen when it was to be carved) and skipped with a Warning, and if none lands the whole bill comes back. Whole floors keep `PlanFloorHomes`, `ExcavateFloor` and the civic floor branch exactly.
- **Save v49.** A floor-shape block straight after D6a's cavern block: the district skeleton (every district's Id, Level and class), then each deep district's Id and sorted points, then each growth project's points (DistrictId, floor, points); the default colony writes 12 bytes. It is read into locals before teardown. Every structural refusal goes through `OutError` ("Save floor-shape block invalid: ..."), never an Error line: a count over its cap or the bytes left, Ids not ascending, a class or level out of range, a shape that names no deep district, a point on a floor outside `-1..-MaxDepth` or reached by no loaded shaft, points out of order or twice, **a level held whole that also holds a shape's point, a point in two districts** (RC 5), a shape that does not start where its district says, a growth point outside its shape. **Attach before the checks (RC 5):** in the district loop each district receives its points before the `IsFloor()` / `FloorsHeld` test, so a Homes shape and an Industry shape on one floor are two deep districts and never meet it. A disagreement left after teardown (a hand-made file) drops the shape or the project with a Warning.
- **Presentation (placeholder; rows K1-K12 of `docs/qa/2026-10-15/gate-d-wording.md`, a fresh prefix).** The DISTRICT tab gains one button, `Underground: whole floor` / `shape, 1 / 2 / 3 floors`: each press steps what a press does below ground. With a shape chosen the district brush anchors a corner, drags a rectangle in the shaft head's lattice and transmits `PaintDistrict` with the height; its ghost runs the sim's own preflight (the Kind's colour, red with the reason, the tip counting points, new, dug, and the district it joins). A queued shape order is drawn on the viewed floor. The floor view washes a shape in its Kind's tint, border and name on every floor it covers (a dug point on its own floor, a point of rock on the sand over it); the growth ghost draws a project's points (dug cells outlined, rock crossed). The CITY panel names a shape. Console: `RH.District shape`, `shapeghost` and `extent`.

**Deviations (the spec was silent or ambiguous; each is the smallest reading).**
1. **Home cells are built lazily, not in `RefreshJobs`.** RefreshJobs does not run on a carve, and RC 6 needs a carve to re-derive the legacy fill. `EnsureDeepHomes` keys on (roster, furniture, zoning, floor cells, districts, `BedAssignVersion`); `RefreshJobs` bumps `BedAssignVersion` whenever it re-derives the bed pieces, so a derivation made between a change and the RefreshJobs that follows it is never kept. `Debug_SetColonistsPerCell` bumps it too.
2. **Two key fields, and the commute key gets them in D6b.** The spec names `DeepCells` for the membership key and a D6c `DeepWalk` for the commute key. The commute flows' district rows read home cells, so `FRHCommuteKey` gets `DeepCells` and `DeepBeds` now (0 with no shape); D6c still adds its own.
3. **The draft's touch rule ("the rectangle must contain or touch a dug cell or a reaching column") is not built.** The merged section says only that every floor in the range must be reached, and that rock may be painted.
4. **A `DeepCells` project works one floor** (the record has one `Level` and `FIntPoint` points): the shallowest floor of the shape with something to offer. The next project of the same shape takes the next floor (tested: a two-floor shape gives a project on -1, then one on -2). `FirstCell` (informational on every older kind) holds the number of leading points that are free cells to zone; the rest are carved.
5. **A deep Civic project is `CivicRoom` with one point** (no new kind); its `Index` stays `INDEX_NONE`.
6. **A trip on a mapped floor is keyed by its two districts too.** The commute keyed a trip by (home floor, work floor), so two shapes on one floor would have collapsed into the first colonist's pair. On a mapped floor a second map keys (floors, home district, work district); the transit split rotates by the floor pair's own running count, so no distance moves; the trip sort breaks ties on the two districts (every other pair of trips is already distinct, so a colony with no shape sorts as before). RC 10 keeps `-deepdistricts` at 60 colonists or fewer, and `GetCommuteFlows` is gated at ramp 0, so the suite cannot exercise this. **It was verified with a temporary probe, since removed:** a colony of 70 (ramp 0.1) with two shapes on one floor gave flows `1->1:1, 1->2:2` and two pairs, a warm read did not rebuild, a carve rebuilt the flows once, and `-RHVerifyCaches` stayed clean.
7. **The block carries the district skeleton** (not in the spec's list) so the cross-checks against the district list run on locals before teardown (RC 5): the district list itself is read after teardown. Two new ceilings, `RH::MaxDeepPoints` 65,536 and `RH::MaxGrowthPoints` 256, back the "counts over the caps" refusal; the paint refuses a total over the first.
8. **The brush offers 1 to 3 floors** (`RH::CavernMaxFloors`, as the cavern brush); the sim accepts up to 8.
9. **DD5 is read as inertness:** a whole-floor district's counts, membership, growth project and finished floor are byte-equal with and without two shapes elsewhere on the colony (the only fair twin; a shape over the same cells grows differently by design).
10. **Harness additions:** `Debug_RemoveColonist` (a departure, for DD3), `Debug_DeepKeys`, `IsShaftColumn`. `RHPlaytest` counts a `DeepCells` project as floor growth in its report (no default run makes one).
11. **The existing suites' literal version expectations moved from 48 to 49** (the same eight places D6a moved from 47 to 48), listed below.

**Tests.** New fails-loudly `-deepdistricts` suite, **150 checks**, in `rh_battery.sh` (now 51 suites and 52 runs), under RC 10's two fixture rules (60 colonists or fewer, ramp 0; no commute distance, long count or flow is printed), on a base colony whose shaft head is **off the 10 m grid** (1370, 1480 cm): DD0 the base and the inert keys; DD1 paint, join, a three-floor shape, a multi-floor join, every refusal, the Value rules (NaN, -3, 0.5, a surface order), whole against shaped in both directions, and the cap (eight 32 x 32 x 8 shapes are 65,536 points, one more is refused); DD2 the frame (a filter at head-frame (1,0) belongs to the shape, `LatticeOf` would read surface cell (2,1) and find nothing, an order given at a world position snaps through the head frame); DD3 home cells against an independent oracle written in the suite: the literal case (an unfurnished cell 0 before a furnished cell 3: residents 1 and 2 in cell 3, 3 in cell 0), CPC 1, 0.5, 0, 1.5, 2 and 0.25, an overfull floor, a departure; DD4 counts, beds and seats per shape against independent walks, an unrated floor; DD5 inertness; DD6 DeepCells (free cells then rock, the ghost equals the carve, no carved point left in `Plan` and the colony saves and loads, an all-rock row where each point leans on the one before, an unrated floor, rock that touches no dig, an Industry shape, a two-floor shape, an erase); DD7 civic (a free cell, then a carve that is not the spiral cell, an unrated floor); DD8 an era project and an agent project finish on the same sol with the same cells; DD9 the save (the layout, a Homes and an Industry shape on one floor with **no Error line counted by a log device**, 19 hand-made file refusals, 6 growth-block refusals, a v48 file refused, and a colony with two open projects equal to a twin that never saved); DD10 the keys (0 with only a whole floor; with a shape both equal the floor cells' version and a carve moves them and the 13th resident of a 12-bed floor gets a cell with no other version moved). **Mutations (each run by hand, restored by hash, `touch`ed and rebuilt):** `IsFloor()` ignoring `Deep` (the old definition) fails 52 of 110 checks (DD1 first); dropping RC 5's attach-before-check ordering fails DD9's two-shapes round trip (the load prints two `LoadColony v37 ... malformed` Error lines, which the counter catches); dropping the `DeepCells` key field fails four DD10 checks.

**Battery.** `bash scripts/sim/rh_battery.sh 10`: `BATTERY COMPLETE: 0 suite(s) not green`, 52 logs, all newer than both dylibs' last change (RedHopeSim 18:55, RedHope 18:36, logs from 18:56), nothing in Source or docs/data newer than `baseline.log`; both pins hold (`power: gen 50 W load 20 W`, `deposit Regolith_A: 40645 kg`). The first run after the sim code was done failed 9 suites for exactly the literal version checks; those were the only failures, and fixing them is the version-line list below. `python3 scripts/sim/diff_battery.py Saved/baselines/battery_post_perf Saved/RHBattery` shows 36 differing lines and the new `deepdistricts.log` (877 sim lines): **34 are the version lines, 17 out and 17 in**, plus 2 wall-clock digits. By suite and check: caverns 4 (CV8 "file says version 48" to 49 with the byte counts 2349 -> 2429 becoming 2361 -> 2441, the 12 bytes of the empty floor-shape block; CV8 v47 refused /48 to /49), deep 6 (K6 version; K6 "a hand-made v48 file" to v49; K6 v46 refused /48 to /49), depth 4 (DP11 version with 2377 -> 2409 becoming 2389 -> 2421; DP11 v46 refused), farice 4 (F16 version "expect 48" to 49 with the refusal's /48 to /49, both on one line; F16 "the v48 save loads" to v49), framing 2 (e5), shafts 2 (SH12), traffic 4 (X15 version, X15 v46 refused), transit 4 (TR18 version, TR18 v46 refused), tunnels 4 (TN1, TN8). The wall-clock digits: `-eraparity` EP4 "119990 substeps in 0.7 s" to 0.6 s (the ledgers on that line are identical).

**`-RHVerifyCaches`.** `deepdistricts` (150 checks), `districts` (118), `citygrowth` (198) and `caverns` (156), each run into a scratch directory (not `Saved/RHBattery`): 0 errors, 0 `RHVERIFY` lines, each completed.

**-scale (outside the battery).** 174 ok, 0 failures, three runs: the five-floor digests do not move (N=5000 `5f9d7352`, the same as D5, D6a and the perf gate; commute digests `5c609384` and `1e609e47` unchanged). The 20-floor budgets, which move with wall clock: cold deep net 2.31 to 2.39 ms (3), 2,500 `PlanDeepRoute` 5.65 to 5.70 ms (10), flows only with 2,000 deep residents 0.637 to 0.645 ms (1); the perf gate measured 2.3, 5.3 and 0.62. No budget moves, and no `-scale` block was added (the spec does not ask for one).

**Playtest.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 d6b_full guide full` is byte-identical to `Saved/RHPlaytest/perf_full.txt` (`cmp` clean, 1,468 lines, 0 sim errors).

**Click test.** `rh_clicktest.sh` passes, 36 of 36, on both runs (the first after the UI was built, the second on the final binary); `shape.tab`, `shape.step`, `shape.height`, `shape.height3`, `shape.whole`, `shape.again` and `shape.drag` are new (the Underground button cycles whole floor, 1, 2, 3, back; the armed brush drags a shape and transmits `PaintDistrict` with Value 1), and `leak.total` counts the shape's drag as the fourth deliberate world click. `REACH 66/66` on the deck.

**Checkpoints and captures.** `rh_checkpoints.sh all --force` re-made Midgame (sol 28.35) and Late (sol 62.35) at v49 (0 sim errors each), and both load in a real-renderer window (`RH.Load Midgame`: sol 28, 25 buildings; `RH.Load Late`: sol 62, 39 buildings; 0 sim errors). Captures in `Saved/RHCapture/d6b`: `d6b_shapes_00..04` (a Demo colony, a 6 x 6 x 3 cavern hall dug): the brush's drag ghost for a one-floor Homes shape (cyan, the tip "3 x 8 cells (30 x 80 m), 24 points (24 new, 18 already dug) - founds a new district"), the ghost for a three-floor Industry shape (crimson, "floors -1 to -3: 5 x 4 cells, 60 points"), floor -1 with both painted (Gale, Homes, teal; Jezero, Industry, crimson; the washes run over the dug hall and the rock beyond it), floor -2 and floor -3 (only Jezero, the three-floor shape, is there: Gale is one floor). `d6b_growth_00..01` (the -scale city, 5,572 people in 5,572 beds, floor -1): a Homes shape over the floor's east edge and the rock past it with its growth project before (a teal wash, four crossed cells on the sand where the carve will open, the label "Gale: 4 new home cells on floor -1, inside the shape - 0%") and after (the banner "finished: Gale: 4 new home cells on floor -1, inside the shape", the strip reading 214 cells where it read 210, a notch of new floor in the shape's edge, the project closed).

**Verification re-run (2026-10-22, after a full-disk window).** The first pass of the gate above ran while `/Volumes/Unreal` and `/` were both nearly full (1.1 GB and 196 MB free), so it was set aside as untrusted and the whole gate was re-run from the same binary (RedHopeSim dylib 21:35, RedHope 21:23; `Build.sh` reports "Target is up to date"). The disks were still tight (`/Volumes/Unreal` 3.2 GB free, `/` 14 GB); `df` was checked before every step and no step failed with a write error. Results: **battery** `BATTERY COMPLETE: 0 suite(s) not green`, 52 logs, pins hold (`power: gen 50 W load 20 W`, `deposit Regolith_A: 40645 kg`); `diff_battery.py` against `battery_post_perf` shows 40 differing lines, **34 the version lines (17 out, 17 in, the list above) and 6 wall-clock digits (3 lines out, 3 in)**: `-arrivals` "the move took 0.05 ms" to 0.06, `-eraparity` EP4 0.7 s to 0.8 s, `-traffic` X16 2.1 s to 2.3 s, nothing else; **playtest** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 rh_playtest.sh 110 d6b_full guide full` is `cmp`-identical to `Saved/baselines/perf_full_playtest.txt` (1,468 lines, 0 sim errors); **`-scale`** three runs, 174 ok and 0 failures each, N=5000 `5f9d7352`, commute digests `5c609384` and `1e609e47`, the 20-floor budgets inside (cold deep net 2.36 to 2.52 ms of 3, 2,500 `PlanDeepRoute` 5.68 to 5.82 ms of 10, flows only with 2,000 deep residents 0.641 to 0.659 ms of 1), no stray `CrashReportClient` running; **click test** 36 of 36, `REACH 65/65`, at **1440x860**; **checkpoints** `rh_checkpoints.sh all --force` re-made Midgame (sol 28.35, 178 playtest lines) and Late (sol 62.35, 399) with 0 sim errors, and both load in a real-renderer window (`RH.Load Midgame`: sol 28, 25 buildings; `RH.Load Late`: sol 62, 39 buildings; 0 sim errors).

**Click test window size.** `rh_clicktest.sh` is hard-coded to 1600x900, which this machine's display (1470x956 points) clamps, so 11 of 36 steps failed on reach (`REACH 29/66`) while every shape step passed. The script now reads `RH_CLICKTEST_RES` (default `1600x900`, unchanged) and **1440x860 is the accepted official size on this machine for now** (`docs/qa-boot-card.md`). The 1600x900 default for captures (`rh_capture.sh`) is untouched.

**Mutations beyond the three above (each run by hand, restored by hash, rebuilt):** home cells ignoring bed pieces fails 13 DD3 checks; dropping the `Plan.Remove` of a carved point fails 2 DD6 checks; the dispatch sending a shape to the surface planner fails 24 DD6 checks.

**Known gaps.** The residents of an overfull shaped floor are counted in no shape and no "Unzoned" figure exists yet. The label over a shape sits at the cell nearest its middle, which can be a point of rock. A shape's air is the floor's (Q-A (a)): nothing in the UI says so beyond the City panel's figures. Commute distances do not use cells until D6c.

### D4 fixes (code review) (2026-10-22, not yet committed)

An independent review of the D4 transit code (read at 41ac355) listed two should-fix defects and two nits. Each was re-found at HEAD (6b5653c) and checked first; one was real, one is not reachable.

**Defect 1, a robot below can stop seeking charge: real, fixed.** A robot on -1 that rides up to charge holds its ride plan (`RobotPaths`) in the sim, and the ride counters advance only while a brain calls `MoveAlongDeep`. When the pad it was riding for is demolished the charge task resets (`Task = FRHTaskFragment()`) with no step taken and the plan stays, boarded and ascending. `DeepChargeTest` reads a boarded ascent as "already going up" and returns Keep for ever, so an idle robot standing on a shaft column never seeks again and runs dry below, where a drained robot is never repaired. Both brains had the same branch (`FRHChargeTask::Tick` in `RHRobotStateTree.cpp`, the Charge case of `RHRobotTaskProcessor.cpp`). The fix is `URHSimWorldSubsystem::ChargePadGone(Entity, PadId)`: it is `!HasBuilding(PadId)` and, when the pad is gone, `DropDeepPath(Entity)`; both brains call it in the pad-gone branch in place of the bare `HasBuilding` test, so a robot that is reset without a step forgets a ride that was for the pad that is gone. The same rule is applied to the two Repair resets in the StateTree brain that also reset without moving (the patient vanished, or is no longer halted); the legacy brain has no Repair. `DropDeepPath` does nothing for a robot with no deep path, so no robot at the surface and no robot with a live route to a standing target is touched. **Check: TR19** (the Charge branch played by the harness through the very call the brains make): an RC-H on -1 seeks, boards the ride up, the ascent is kept while the pad stands and the ride is untouched; the pad is demolished through the real Demolish order and a second pad built; after the reset the ride is gone, the charge test seeks again toward the second pad, and the robot rides up and docks with charge left. With `DropDeepPath` taken out of `ChargePadGone` TR19's reset check fails (the test reads Keep from the stale ascent); restored by hash it passes.

**Defect 2, the reserve prices a two-lift route's descent at a frozen wait: not reachable, not changed.** The pricing is as the review read it: `DeepReserveAt` prices every ascent hold at `MaxWaitSec` and each descent hold at the wait read when the route was planned. But the planner never picks a route with a descent when its end is a surface pad, which is the only end the reserve plans. Every route that walks a floor from one shaft's column to another's costs at least what the surface leg between the two heads costs (the lattice walk is 1,000 + the walk + 1,000 cm, never under the straight line between the columns, and a road only makes the surface leg cheaper), and a descent adds a ride down and a second ride up, each with its dwell and wait, so the route that rides up the first shaft and crosses on the surface is always strictly cheaper than the one that rides down it, crosses below and rides up the second. **Guard: TR20** plans 2,985 routes from every carved cell of 16 layouts (two fitted shafts, tunnels joining them on both floors, roads, crowded shafts at 120 and 600 s) to random surface pads; none descends (0 of 2,985), and 1,077 of them cross a tunnel from shaft 0's dig to ride shaft 1, so the sweep does reach the case. If a later change to the planner (a floor-to-floor shortcut, a surface cost that can exceed the walk) makes a descent possible, TR20 fails and the reserve must price every not-yet-boarded wait at `MaxWaitSec` (REVISION item 7) at the same time. TR20 has no "before": there was no defect to fix.

**The nits.**
- A ride counted twice across a save: the sentence in the D4 section above said one place of over-count at most. A load clears every robot's trip (`RobotPaths.Reset()`), so each robot in a hold or a ride at the save re-plans from its column, boards again and charges its cargo places again: up to 2 places for each hauler in flight at the save (a 200 kg load), 0 for a robot carrying nothing. The sentence is corrected. The same words stand in `docs/qa/2026-10-21/d4-transit-design.md` (the design record) and are left as the record of what was cleared.
- A hand-off from mid-floor when a below-ground hauler's destination is demolished, with no mass lost: not changed. What the code does today is `StepHandoffHold` ending the hold at once and `HaulUnload` finding no building, which books the load as `HaulLostKg` (the mass balance counts it as lost, as a hauler's load has always been) and completes the task. The nit's wording was not specific enough to say whether it asks for a test that the balance holds in that case or for the load to be kept; either is a design call or a new test case, not a trivial inert fix, so it is left for the director.

### D6c deep commute: real commutes between digs: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-23, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-22/d6-caverns-design.md`, section 3 of the MERGED DESIGN AND REVISION (RC 6, 9, 10 and 11 apply), in the order of authority the director set: the DIRECTOR ANSWERs first (**Q-A (a):** reach, air and rating stay floor-wide, so a walk joins digs for people without joining their air; **Q-C (a):** trams carry people only, which is D6d), then the REVISION, then the review. D6c adds no `docs/data` row and no DataTable, **no save bump** (no state is added and nothing derived is saved; the file is the same bytes before and after the commute is read, DC9), and no new Mass fragment. Hand-play checkpoints made at v49 stay valid: nothing in the format moved.

**What D6c builds.**
- **The switch is the first shape on a floor.** On a *mapped* floor (one that holds a D6b floor shape) a resident is measured from their **home cell** and a seat from its **cell** (a cell seat `(L, i, 0)` is cell i; a module seat is the cell under its building). Every other floor and every surface trip runs today's lines exactly: the head anchor, D2's cheapest shaft, D4's split. D6c is inert while no floor is mapped, and under the 60-160 ramp (below 61 colonists nothing is derived: no flows, no network).
- **The walk network (`FRHWalkFloor`, new `RHSimDeepWalk.cpp`), derived per mapped floor, never saved.** Compartments are the 4-connected components of carved cells that are not tunnel chain points; a person walks the octile line inside one (`1000 x max(|dx|,|dy|) + 414 x min(|dx|,|dy|)` cm, integers: straight-line inside a dig) and a tunnel cell by cell, 1,000 cm a step. The nodes are the chain cells, the **doors** (a compartment cell 4-adjacent to a carved chain cell) and the **landings** (the lowest-ordinal carved 4-neighbour of a reaching shaft's column); one integer Dijkstra per door and landing, ties to the lower node index (a chain cell an end stands on gets its own, lazily). Cells carved later (a tunnel's last cell, a cavern) move the network through `FloorCellsVersion`.
- **A trip** (`MeasureDeepTrip`) is priced **wait-free** and the waits are added after, so a crowd lengthens a trip and never flips its route from sol to sol (DC3: a 600 s wait on shaft 0, 60,000 cm against a 4,067 cm lead, and the resident stays on shaft 0, this sol and the next). Same floor: the walk, or out by one shaft and down another when that is shorter (so two unjoined digs on one floor are no longer 0 m: **R2 is fixed for shaped floors**, 47,015 cm in DC1 against D4's 0 with no shape). Floor to surface: the minimum over the landed shafts of walk + ride + the surface leg from that shaft's head. Floor to floor: the minimum over shafts landed on both floors, else two shafts through the surface. Ties go to the lower Id. `ShaftPeople` gains one per shaft ridden (a dig-to-dig trip loads two).
- **Off the net** (an unmapped floor at either end, a resident with no home cell, a cell with no door path to a landing - D3 R6): the **whole trip** keeps D6b's head anchor and the D4 split, and is counted (`GetCommuteOffNet()`, per colonist; `GetCommuteWalked()` counts the rest). No trip is left unmeasured.
- **Keys (RC 6).** `FRHCommuteKey` gains `DeepTunnels` (`TunnelsVersion`) and `DeepShafts` (`ShaftsVersion`), both 0 for ever while no floor is mapped, beside D6b's `DeepCells` and the district version; the X13 and X14 rebuild counts cannot move. The trip is keyed by its two cells (`FRHCommuteTrip` gains `HomeCell`, `WorkCell`, `ShaftId2`), and the trip sort breaks its last ties on (home cell, seat cell) (RC 11): several trips share one head anchor and both districts and differ only in their cells.
- **Two derived memos, both inside the walk network (so both die with it):** the walk from a cell to each shaft's landing (asked once a cell and a shaft), and the wait-free answer of a trip, dropped when the cars, the built roads or the commute tuning move. Under `-RHVerifyCaches` a reused memo entry is re-measured and compared, and `IsDeepWalkFresh` re-derives the network and compares it, the flows' own check now includes it.

**Deviations (the spec was silent or ambiguous; each is the smallest reading).**
1. **Two key fields, not one composite `DeepWalk`.** The spec allows "separate fields or a strict combination, so no two bumps collide" (D4's precedent): separate fields.
2. **Door-to-door edges inside a compartment.** The spec says one Dijkstra per door over doors and chain points; a route that runs dig, tunnel, dig, tunnel, dig has to cross the middle dig, so the graph also joins every two doors of one compartment by their octile line.
3. **Off the net takes the whole trip**, not only the end that has no cell: a trip that touches an unmapped floor keeps the D6b/D4 price with the mapped end at the head anchor, so a colony's unmapped floors measure byte for byte as before (DC6).
4. **R4's alert is not built.** The risk list says the first shape on a floor changes that floor's commutes and that "its alert says so, as Gate-D placeholder copy". Any sim-side alert would add a log line to `deepdistricts.log`, which the gate holds to "new log only", and the build list for D6c names no alert. No player-facing wording was added, so `gate-d-wording.md` has no new row. Decide whether the shape brush should say it (presentation side, no sim line).
5. **Same-floor, one shaft up and down again is never a candidate**: it is never shorter than the walk to that shaft's landing, and the walk is already in the minimum.
6. **`FRHCommutePair` (the presentation readout) gains `HomeCell`, `WorkCell`, `ShaftId` and `ShaftId2`.** Additive; nothing in `RedHope` reads them yet. A walked trip's anchors are the heads of the shafts it rides (a trip that rides none anchors at shaft 0's head), so the shuttle overlay draws as before; a trip that goes up one shaft and down another draws no surface leg between the heads (D4's floor-to-floor trips did not either).
7. **`Debug_ReroutePosition` also forgets the walk network**, so the harness's "cold" read is cold all the way down.
8. **Stairs and open volumes are not built** (D7); people change floors by shaft only, as the REVISION says.
9. **Flows-only, not a spec budget.** A trip on a mapped floor is a pair of *cells*, so 4,800 residents are about 4,800 distinct trips to measure from memory and sort (about 0.4 ms of 1.1 ms is the sort); the unshaped scale colonies have a few hundred. The `-scale` block guards it at 3 ms and **reports 1.13 ms against the 1 ms line of the unshaped colonies: a finding for the director, not a budget edit.**

**Tests.** New fails-loudly `-deepcommute` suite, **55 checks**, in `rh_battery.sh` (now 52 suites and 53 runs). Every distance is by hand or from an independent oracle (a Floyd-Warshall over every carved cell, sharing no code with the sim). DC0 the inert keys; **DC1** hand integers (one compartment: 2,414 and 3,828 cm; across a 30-cell tunnel: 34,828 cm; between unjoined digs through two shafts: 47,015 cm, and the same layout with no shape: 0, so the switch is the first shape; door to door 31,000, end to end 29,000), then a sweep of **every (home, seat) pair of two floors against the oracle** (864 and 324 trips, 864 and 180 joined on foot); **DC2** floor to surface picks walk plus ride (12 residents ride shaft 0 and 6 shaft 1; the resident of (35,2) walks 1,000 cm and rides shaft 1 although the workplace stands 29 cm nearer head 0; a surface home to a floor seat is the same trip backwards); **DC3** a crowded shaft does not flip the route; **DC4** `ShaftPeople` per shaft ridden (12 and 6; a floor-to-floor trip rides the one shaft both floors share, once; a dig-to-dig trip names both); **DC5** off the net (two residents past the last bed; a seat on an unmapped floor; a lone dug cell with no door path to a landing) and two trips that differ only in their cells sort the same in both build orders; **DC6** thirty residents of an unmapped floor measure byte for byte as in the twin with no shape; **DC7** the ramp at 60 (no flows, no network built), 61 (0.01), 160 and 161; **DC8** the era band and the agent band measure the same 40 trips; **DC9** a tunnel one cell short of joining the digs is still two shafts, the 30th cell joins them and the flows and network rebuild once, the keys, the file is the same bytes before and after the commute is read, and a save loads back.

**Mutations (each run by hand, restored by hash, `touch`ed and rebuilt):** each fails its check. The trip sort without the cell tie-break: DC5 (1 of 55). The route choice reading waits (memo bypassed, so the wait is read): DC3's crowd (1). Octile 414 to 400: 11 (DC1 first). A tunnel step of 100 cm: 9. Floor to surface ignoring the walk: 6 (DC2, DC3). The second shaft not loaded: DC4 (1). The switch on every floor (`bShapeTrip` = any shape): DC6 (1, 30 off the net for 10). The ramp gate off in `GetColonistCommuteCm`: DC7 (1). The walk network's key ignoring the floor's cells: DC9 (1, the 30th cell stays 47,015). A landing that is the highest ordinal, not the lowest: 13.

**Battery.** `bash scripts/sim/rh_battery.sh 10`: `BATTERY COMPLETE: 0 suite(s) not green`, 53 logs, all newer than both dylibs (RedHopeSim 00:49, RedHope 00:28, baseline log 00:52), nothing in Source or `docs/data` newer than `baseline.log`; both pins hold (`power: gen 50 W load 20 W`, `deposit Regolith_A: 40645 kg`). `python3 scripts/sim/diff_battery.py Saved/baselines/battery_post_d4fix Saved/RHBattery` shows **only the new `deepcommute.log` (1,145 sim lines) and two wall-clock digits** (`-arrivals` ARRV e "0.06 ms" to 0.04; `-traffic` X16 "2.2 s" to 2.1 s): no save-version line moved, because nothing was bumped, and `deepdistricts.log` is line-identical (RC 10 held).

**`-RHVerifyCaches`.** `deepcommute` (55 checks), `districts`, `transit`, `tunnels` and `deepdistricts`, each into a scratch directory: 0 errors, 0 `RHVERIFY` lines, each completed.

**-scale (outside the battery).** 180 ok, 0 failures, two runs (174 before D6c plus the six new lines): every digest line is identical to a run of HEAD's binary, and the existing 20-floor lines are where they were (cold deep net 2.3 to 2.4 ms, `PlanDeepRoute` 5.1 to 5.4 ms, cold commute 5.9 ms, flows only 0.65 ms with 2,000 deep residents). **New block, run last, on the 20-floor colony: all 20 floors mapped (the five whole-floor districts erased first: a floor is whole or shaped), a 26-cell tunnel carved on every floor, 4,799 colonists re-homed across them, 30% sitting in floor cells, transit on:** cold walk network 0.96 to 1.33 ms (budget 5), cold commute with the network, the position memo and the trips 8.1 to 8.3 ms (budget 10), warm read 0.00001 ms (0.01), flows only 1.13 ms (see deviation 9), 4,799 walked and none off the net. The walk-network figure is for the whole of twenty floors; the memo of walks and trips is why a re-derived trip is a lookup.

**Playtest.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 d6c_full guide full` is byte-identical to `Saved/baselines/d6b_full_playtest.txt` (`cmp` clean, 1,468 lines, 0 sim errors, the same sha256). `-RHCommuteAudit` on that playtest (also byte-identical): peak population 115, peak ramp 0.55, longest commute 12,000 cm, 0 long commuters, margin 18,000 cm below the 30,000 cm line (no earlier figure was recorded to compare; nothing on that path changed, since no floor is mapped).

**Click test.** Not run: nothing in `RedHope` changed (no panel, brush, string or tab), and the click test is the bar only for a UI change.

**Known gaps.** The walk is straight-line inside a compartment, so a concave painted compartment (a U-shaped hall) is undercounted (R2 of the REVISION's risk list); tunnels are walked exactly. A cell with no door path to a landing is only reachable in a fixture today (a lone cell dug through the plan order), since every dig grows from a shaft or off a dig. D6d adds `TramVersion` to the walk network's key and the tram edge to its graph.

### D8 the deep view: the section beside the floor view: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-23, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-23/d8-deep-view-design.md`, in the order of authority the director set: the DIRECTOR ANSWER first (Q-B (a): people below ground are pips, with commuter arrows; Q-C (a): D8 builds now on HEAD plus D6c, and the tram band, the atrium visuals and the served-capacity bar ship inside D6d, D7 and D6e; Q-D (a): the thin ant farm, every floor three rows deep at 8 degrees; Q-A dropped, "beside the floor view" logged as deviation 1), then the REVISION's R1-R9 and O1-O5, then the draft. Presentation only: **no file under `Source/RedHopeSim` changed** (`git diff --stat -- Source/RedHopeSim` is empty), no save field, no checkpoint remake.

**What D8 builds.**
- **The section** (`URHSectionVisualizerSubsystem`, new, a pure listener). A vertical cut on a lattice row through the chosen shaft's column, the camera looking along one of four lattice axes; every bored floor stacked at its real depth, `rh.SectionDepth` rows (3) drawn on and behind the cut, a dark rock card closing the slab, a 1 m crust at Z = 0 carrying the shaft heads and the surface buildings in the drawn rows. Per floor by band from the focus: within one floor, plates tinted by room and district, wall strips, building and furniture blocks, resident pips; within five, plates and walls; further, plates tinted by D5's depth and Warmth only. The shaft columns, the lift cars (a presentation phase, at most two floors a real second), the cavern frames (clipped to the drawn rows) and the floor labels at every band. Unlit: one `M_Graybox` emissive colour per component, no light, no shadow, no collision; picks are analytic.
- **One view state (R1).** `ARHPlayerController` owns it (`bSectionOn`, `SectionFocus`, `SectionAxis`, `SectionPlaneRow`, `SectionShaft`) and pushes the mirror to the colony visualizer (`SetSectionMode`) and the section (`SetView`). `ActiveLevel` stays frozen at the entry floor; the slider, the strip rows, PageUp/PageDown (Shift: five floors), Home and End move only the focus. Every colony visibility site goes through `ShowsLevel`, `ShowsSurface` and `ShowsUnderground`, which are today's expressions with the section off; with it on, every colony actor, the environment rig and MarsGround are hidden. Crew draws no one (Q-B (a)); audio plays the focus floor's ambience; atmosphere reads as underground; roads, traffic, rovers and the surface robot slice hide; the district, cavern, tunnel and growth visualizers hide their holder; robots below are drawn only inside the slab (`IsInSlab`).
- **Input (R2).** `V` toggles (ignored over a menu); Escape closes a menu first, then the section, then opens the pause menu; Q/E turn a quarter on the press (`IA_Orbit` Started), the held orbit and RMB/MMB drags are locked; W/S step the plane a row on the press and repeat after 0.4 s every 0.25 s; A/D pan along the plane; R/F tilt inside 5-35 degrees through a pawn override (`ARHStrategyPawn::SetSectionView`, `GetCameraPose`, `SolveZoomForWidthCm`), so the section's pitch no longer follows the zoom. Arming any mode leaves the section onto the focus floor with the mode armed (one choke point in `TickSection`); any other `SetActiveLevel` caller leaves it at that floor.
- **Picking (R4).** `OnClick` counts the click, then in the section only picks: the ray meets the cut face, the floor is the hit's height, the cell is the first carved one behind the cut on that column, and the click dives (leaves the section on that floor, the camera on that cell). No order path is reachable.
- **The deck.** **Section** under the SHAFT header (lit and `Section [on]` while on); a vertical depth slider beside the rows while the section is on (tagged `RH.SectionSlider`); `GetShownLevel` (the focus in the section) for the lit row, row visibility, the scroll follow, the lift card, the tunnel and cavern lists; **Cut here** on every listed cavern (in the section the list follows the focus and shows finished caverns too); the TRAFFIC legend's below-ground text in the section.
- **TRAFFIC below ground (section 4).** At 2 Hz: a bar beside each fitted shaft (`GetShaftPeopleLoad + GetShaftCargoLoad` over `GetShaftCapacityPlaces`, coloured by `GetTransitWaitSec` against `GetMaxWaitSec`, labelled "nominal" until D6e); per floor an out-arrow and an in-arrow summed from `GetCommutePairs` between floors; per tunnel the robots the agent visualizer drew on its chain. "No commuting yet" below the ramp.
- **Measurement (R8).** `RHFrameStats::Section` (a third slot), the `[FrameStats]` line's section tick, counts and robots drawn; `RH.DeepBench N` (public sim calls only); `RH.Section <on [axis]|off|focus N|axis K|plane R|cut Id|check flows>`; `rh_capture.sh` passes `RH_CAPTURE_EXTRA` before `-ExecCmds`.

**Deviations.**
1. **The section sits beside the floor view** (former Q-A).
2. **The tint is the R5 (a) fallback, a fixed 512-colour palette** (8 levels a channel), one plain ISM per colour, all made on the first entry with the label pools (21.8 to 26.1 ms, once). There is no Unreal MCP server in this session to author `M_SectionTint`. A component made mid-view stalled a frame for about 30 ms in the bench, which is why nothing is created after entry. Each colour re-uploads only when its instances change (a per-colour signature).
3. **Rebuild keys.** Population, jobs, bed assignment and furniture are a separate "people" key: when only it moves, only the full-detail floors redraw, at most once a second (a city of thousands moves them every few seconds). No private sim version is read: the room tints follow the colony visualizer's `GetPropsVersion` with the public versions.
4. **The camera framing.** The focus sits on the cut face, centred on the dig, and the fitted width is the larger of the carved extent plus six cells a side for the labels and arrows (plus 10%), the whole bored stack through the viewport's aspect, and 120 m (the spec's "extent plus 10%" put a three-cell dig 30 m from the camera, with half its cut face off screen). Entry lands square at once; later quarter turns ease.
5. **`RH.DeepBench`, where it differs from R8.** Shafts 1 and 2 carve a 10-cell landing on each floor, not a 210-cell spiral: a floor's circulation replaces at most about 240 cells of leak (480 kg a sol against 2 kg a cell), so three full spirals unrated every floor, closed every seat and bed, and the colony emptied. The city's floors are re-rated after the landings. Stations come from `RefreshJobs` (it re-derives every station each run, so `Debug_SetColonistStation` does not survive); 70 Workstation cells are zoned on each of -6..-20 so the flows check has its 500 cross-floor commuters. The three shafts stand on row y = 0, so the cut through all of them is axis 1 (`RH.Section on 1`), and the 60 robots stand on rows y = 0..2.
6. **Runs D and E are measured in adjacent pairs** (D0 then D1, E0 then E1, each pair seconds apart), not in R8's order: across three minutes the bench city drifts (commuting starts, people leave) and a +4 ms move showed up that was the city, not the section.
7. **Deferred under Q-C (a):** the tram band (D6d), the served-capacity bar (D6e), the atrium void, light, facing edge and `section.cut.atrium` (D7). The "Required by D8" accessors are in the design doc for those builders.
8. Tunnel robot counts are sampled from the agent visualizer's drawn positions with `GetTunnels()` for the chains, not a sim accessor.

**Tests.**
- **Click test** (`rh_clicktest.sh` at 1440x860): 65 checks, among them `section.setup`, `section.unit` (the slab helpers on four axes, the rows and the LOD bands), `section.on`, `sweep.section` (REACH 68/68, the slider counted at its centre and both ends), `section.row`, `section.slider` and `.mid`, `section.key`, `.home`, `.end`, `.shift`, `.shift.up`, `.turn`, `.turn.back`, `.turn.held` (exactly one turn), `.step`, `.step.back`, `.step.held` (the repeat), `.locks.rmb`, `.locks.mmb`, `.cut`, `.traffic`, `.traffic.off`, `.esc.menu`, `.esc`, `.V`, `.V.off`, `.dive`, `.arm`, and `leak.total` with the dive as the fifth deliberate world click. New harness primitives: `SendKeyDown/Up` and `PushKey` (Shift and hold), `PushButtonDrag` (RMB, MMB), `LocateTagged`, `LocateFloorRow`, `LocateSectionPlate`, `WorldPointToSlate`.
- **Live smoke** (`RH_CAPTURE_EXTRA=-RHVerifyCaches`, `RH.DeepBench 3000`, the section and TRAFFIC on, three sols at 8x): 0 `RHVERIFY` lines; `[SectionCheck] flows: 600 cross-floor commuters in 33 floor pairs, matches=1` at the start and `554 ... in 12 floor pairs, matches=1` after three sols; 898 overlay refreshes.

**The frame check** (`RH.DeepBench 3000`, 15 s `RH.FrameStats` windows, TSR as shipped, the final build; median / p95 ms; R8's budgets):

| Run | 1600x900 | 1440x860 | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|
| D0 surface, TRAFFIC on, never entered | 28.12 / 28.74 | 28.72 / 29.33 | baseline |
| D1 the same, just after entering and leaving | 28.23 / 28.96 (+0.11) | 28.78 / 29.32 (+0.06) | within 1.0 of D0 |
| E0 floor -1, never entered | 48.09 / 50.99 | 48.75 / 52.36 | baseline |
| E1 the same, just after entering and leaving | 47.90 / 51.22 (-0.19) | 48.68 / 52.39 (-0.07) | within 1.0 of E0 |
| A section, focus -10 | 26.94 / 27.41 | 27.70 / 28.36 | 33 / 45 |
| A section, focus -3 (furnished) | 26.48 / 27.17 | 27.23 / 27.78 | 33 / 45 |
| B section and TRAFFIC, focus -10 | 26.94 / 27.61 | 27.70 / 28.34 | 35 / 48 |
| C section, focus -1 / -20 | 26.22 / 26.88 | 26.97 / 27.55 | 33 / 45 |

The section's slot: 0.000 ms a frame with the section off (D, E) and 0.019 to 0.023 ms with it on; the worst rebuild in the windows 2.58 ms (1600x900) and 3.13 ms (1440x860), against 8; 1,260 plates, 12 cars and 60 robots drawn; 33 floor pairs and 600 cross-floor commuters in `[SectionCheck] ... matches=1 | PASS` in both sessions. The first entry makes the palette and the label pools once: 22.8 to 28.8 ms.

**Click test.** Three runs on the final build at 1440x860: 65 / 65, `CLICKTEST VERDICT: PASS` each time.

**Playtest.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 d8_full guide full` is byte-identical to `Saved/RHPlaytest/d6c_full.txt` (`cmp` clean, 1,468 lines, 0 sim errors). The playtest runs this module, so the section's subsystem, the new deck widgets and every new branch were live and idle in it.

**Battery.** `bash scripts/sim/rh_battery.sh 10`: `BATTERY COMPLETE: 0 suite(s) not green`, logs newer than both dylibs, nothing in `Source` or `docs/data` newer than `baseline.log`; both pins hold (`power: gen 50 W load 20 W`, `deposit Regolith_A: 40645 kg`). `diff_battery.py Saved/baselines/battery_post_d6c Saved/RHBattery`: 4 differing lines, all wall-clock digits (`eraparity.log` EP4 0.7 s to 0.6 s, `traffic.log` X16 2.1 s to 2.0 s). No version line moves: D8 has no save change.

**Captures** (`Saved/RHCapture/d8/keep`, 1600x900, real renderer, looked at): `d8_1_entry_three_shafts.png` (the cut on row y = 0 through all three columns, every floor stacked, the slider beside the rows), `d8_2_focus-10_lod0.png` (pips and blocks on -9..-11, plates elsewhere, warm floors amber, cyan cars in the columns), `d8_3_cut_through_cavern.png` (Cut here: the plane through the cavern's middle row, its teal frame over -8..-10), `d8_5_traffic_lift_bars_and_flows.png` (lift bars and labels "Shaft 1 · 400 / 4,000 places (nominal) · wait 0 s · 200 commuters a sol", each floor's out and in arrows, the below-ground legend), `d8_6_deck_slider_focus-6.png` (the slider and the lit -6 row), `d8_7_after_leaving_floor-2.png` (the ordinary floor view of -2). Captures 4 (an atrium) and the tram part of 5 wait for D7 and D6d (Q-C (a)).

**Known gaps.** The upper floor labels are low-contrast where they cross the rock card, and at 1600x900 the rightmost shaft's label can sit under the right column. The RMB and MMB lock checks pass, but a synthetic drag may produce no mouse delta in the game's input, so they show the camera did not move, not that a real drag was refused. The bench city is not self-sustaining (it loses people over sols), so each frame window states its population. Single frames over 150 ms in the bench line up with the scheduled screenshots (the section's slot reads 0.0 in each).

### D6d trams with people: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-23, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-22/d6-caverns-design.md`, section 4 of the MERGED DESIGN AND REVISION (RC 9 applies), in the order of authority the director set: the DIRECTOR ANSWERs first (**Q-C (a):** trams carry people only; robots never ride), and from the "Required by D8 in D6d's build" list in `docs/qa/2026-10-23/d8-deep-view-design.md` (R7). This gate was started by one builder session, which stopped mid-way; a second session took it over from the uncommitted tree (backed up in `Saved/backups/d6d-wip-1023-1231`), re-checked it item by item against the spec, fixed one test literal, and ran the gate.

**What D6d builds.**
- **`LayTramLine`** (`Value` = a tunnel Id; `RH.LayTram`): refused, each with its reason, when there is no lift car, the tunnel is unknown, cancelled or unfinished, its chain is under `TramMinCells`, it already has a line, `MaxTramLines` are laid, or the Struct bill (`TramStructKgPerCell` x the chain, through `TakeStructForBill`) is short.
- **`FRHTramLine`** (saved): Id, RegionId, Level, TunnelId, the two stops as lattice points (never ordinals), WaitSec and PrevWaitSec. A line maps its floor (D6c's switch) without being a shape.
- **The edge.** Between the two stops in the people's walk network (`FRHDeepWalk`), never in the robots' deep net: `(TramHeadwaySec / 2 + ceil(length / speed)) x CommuteWaitCmPerSec`, wait-free; the line's frozen `WaitSec` is added after the route is chosen. The walk network's and the commute's keys gain `TramVersion`.
- **Capacity.** In the transit roll, after the shafts, in ascending line Id: load `2 x TramPeople x ramp`, the lifts' wait formula against `TramPlacesPerSol`.
- **The accessors D8 asked for:** `GetTramLines`, `GetTramPeople`, `GetTramVersion`; the commute's `-RHVerifyCaches` compare includes `TramPeople`.
- **Presentation:** a tram visualizer (one capsule per line by phase, hidden while the section is on), the section's tram band (each line along its tunnel, coloured by its wait, riders in the legend), the deck's TRAMS card and the Tram brush; `RH.DeepBench N trams` lays a line on each bench tunnel.
- **Save v50.** A tram block straight after the floor-shape block (NextTramId, the count, then 40 bytes a line; the default colony writes 8 bytes), read into locals before teardown, with structural refusals through `OutError`.
- **Rows** (placeholders, director review; CSV and DT synced): `TramMinCells` 12, `MaxTramLines` 8 (cap 32), `TramStructKgPerCell` 20, `TramHeadwaySec` 60, `TramSpeedMps` 10, `TramPlacesPerSol` 600.

**Deviation.** The TM6 round-trip check expected the reloaded trip to carry the line's *previous* wait (456 s); the spec adds the frozen `WaitSec` (240 s) after the route is chosen, and that is what both the saving and the loaded colony measure (11,728 + 240 x 100 = 35,728 cm). The literal was corrected to the frozen wait; the code was not changed.

**Tests.** New `-trams` suite, 89 checks, in `rh_battery.sh` (now 53 suites and 54 runs): TM0 data and inertness, TM1 every refusal, TM2 the edge integer, TM3 riders and the ramp (60, 61, 160), TM4 the wait applied after the choice with no oscillation, TM5 a line maps its floor (and is no shape), TM6 the save (layout, an 8-byte default, a byte-equal round trip, every structural refusal, the v49 refusal), TM7 era and agent roll alike at the sol boundary, TM8 inert with no line, order-free riders, the caches fresh, and robots never ride. Every step pins the bank to 1e9 (RC 9). **Mutations**, each run by hand and restored by hash: dropping the headway from the edge fails 26 checks (first TM2); dropping the factor of 2 from the load fails 18 (first TM3).

**Battery.** `rh_battery.sh 10`: 0 suites not green; both pins hold. Against `battery_post_d8`: 46 differing lines and the new `trams.log`. Apart from the save-version lines (49 to 50), they are wall-clock digits (`arrivals` e, `eraparity` EP4, `traffic` X16) and one expected byte count: `deepcommute` DC9's save file is 8 bytes longer (3,710 to 3,718), the empty tram block.

**-scale** (outside the battery). The first run, straight after the battery, missed four wall-clock budgets by small margins (two of them in code D6d does not touch); two reruns in isolation were clean, 0 errors, digests unchanged (N=5000 `5f9d7352`): a cold commute with transit, 20 mapped floors and 4,799 colonists 8.6 ms, and with twenty tram lines 9.2 ms (budget 10, a thin margin); a cold deep net 2.44 ms (3); StepPower with fifteen cooling entries 0.043 ms (0.05).

**Playtest.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 d6d_full guide full` is byte-identical to `Saved/RHPlaytest/d8_full.txt` (1,468 lines, 0 sim errors).

**Click test.** Three runs at 1440x860: 72 / 72 each (`tram.setup`, `tram.unit`, `tram.tool`, `tram.land`, `tram.laid`, `tram.capsule`, `tram.moves` are new).

**Checkpoints and captures.** `rh_checkpoints.sh all --force` re-made Midgame (sol 28.35) and Late (sol 62.35) at v50, 0 sim errors. Captures in `Saved/RHCapture/d6d/keep` (`RH.DeepBench 3000 trams`): the section with both tram bands on -4 and -14 and the TRAMS card; the section at -14; the floor view of -4.

**Known gaps.** The bench's tram lines carry 0 riders (their tunnels lead to rock with no homes or seats). The floor-view capture does not frame the tunnel, so the capsule is shown by the click test, not a picture. The TRAFFIC legend's below-ground text, with the tram lines added, runs over the SHAFT panel. The tram-lines `-scale` budget has about 0.8 ms of headroom.

### E1 the ledger: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-23, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-22/e1-ledger-design.md` in the order of authority the director set: the DIRECTOR ANSWER (**Q-A (a):** one Earth reference value per resource, used both ways, `EarthValue_mcr`; **Q-B (a):** recycled water is netted, not produced), then REVISION R1-R9, then the PROFESSOR REVIEW, then the design.

**What E1 builds.**
- **A second ledger beside C5d's**, read by nothing but the `-economy` suite: no sim rule, panel, cheat or log line reads it, and C5d's `ProdAccKg` / `ProdRing` / `NoteProduced` / `GetProductionPerSol` are untouched. One slot per mass resource (18: every `RH_Resources` row but Power, Research and Hope), sorted by name with a case-sensitive ordinal compare. Four legs a slot: **Made**, **Used**, **In**, **Out**. Each leg has an open kg for the current sol, an 8-sol ring of whole grams and a lifetime total in grams. `RollEconomyLedger` runs straight after `RollProductionLedger` on the same sol threshold; the sub-gram residual carries over (under half a gram), and a long jump records the open sol and then empty sols, 8 at most.
- **One hook, `NoteFlow(Resource, Leg, Kg)`**, at about 45 sites in seven files. It records only what really moved (a value already computed, or a before-minus-after read), ignores Kg <= 0 and names with no slot, and is never in `AddStock`. The sites: recipe inputs (Used, at batch start) and outputs (Made, at completion); the Borer's hydrogen; floor and hab leaks and breathing (one call per step); eating (`DrawPool` gains an out-param) and Luxury; greywater, Used = drawn - returned (Q-B (a); `DrawPool` records no water); the garden (Soil, Seeds, Glass, crop water, Fertilizer Used; Compost, O2, Food Made); the crisis drain; construction completion (held before - held after); Furnish, Fit and Launder through `SpendTradeLot`, which gains an optional `OutSpent` so the caller records what each line actually took; roads, lift cars and tram lines through `TakeStructForBill`; the road refund; sales and the salvage share only (`RemoveBuilding` records the credited total less what the building held); the agent dig (`DigDeposit`) and the era dig (to a hopper, to a store); growth and shaft spoil chemicals (R3); the haul losses (R2); robot repair; the convoy (Used H2 and wear, Out the rival's lot; In its lot home); covert loot (In); a ship landing's mass items, the crew pod's and transport's food and the Big Arrival's provisions (In); the discovery reward (Made).
- **R2.** `NoteHaulCargoLost(FName, double)`; `HaulUnload` gains a trailing `CargoResource` (not part of the rover test) that the four robot callers pass; a loss with no name adds to `UnslottedLostG` (saved, read only by tests).
- **R8.** `CompleteGrowthProject` gains `FRHGrowthApplied* OutApplied` (`Credited` from every `CreditLander` of unplaced pieces, `AirO2Kg` from every write into air); at completion each bill line records Used = bill - credited - air (oxygen only), floored at 0. A refused project records nothing; the bill taken at the start is a move into escrow.
- **Whole grams at the trade boundary**: `RoundToGrams` on the rivals' `ImportLot` / `ExportLot` (convoy, launder, convoy home), the covert amount and `ScaleQuotaBill`'s round-0 return. An identity on every authored value (EC2).
- **Data.** `RH_Resources` gains `EarthValue_mcr` (int32 milli-credits per kg; per unit for HabSegment), the design's placeholder table; CSV and `DT_Resources` synced; each Notes cell says "EarthValue placeholder, director review." (Hope: "Hope is never valued (ruling 11).").
- **Save v51.** The E1 block is the last thing in the file: the slot count, then per slot its name and per leg the open kg, the lifetime grams and the 8 ring entries; then fill, head, checked sol and the unslotted loss. Its size depends only on the slot table (5,997 bytes today). R1: the block is never refused; a malformed one (unsorted or repeated name, negative ring entry, fill or head out of range, cut short) resets the ledger to empty with a **Warning**, and a name with no slot is skipped with a Warning. No path logs an Error.

**EC7, the paired run, measured first (R5, R6).** Every barred leg passed the 5% bar on the first prototype build; nothing was re-tuned.
- **EC7a, dig and Forge** (EP4's colony and harness), agent / era over 10 sols: Regolith Made 6,756.574 / 6,790.323 kg (+0.50%), ring 4,800.000 / 4,838.710 (+0.80%, the widest); Regolith Used 6,000 / 6,000 (0.00%); Struct Made 1,475 / 1,475 (0.00%).
- **EC7b, life support** (no robots): Oxygen Made 1,059.100 / 1,059.100 and Used 277.875 / 277.875; Water Made 10,530 / 10,485 (-0.43%) and Used 1,295.550 / 1,295.550; Food Made 23.406 / 23.390 (-0.07%) and Used 36.270 / 36.270; Hydrogen Made 130.900 / 130.900; Ice Used 11,750 / 11,700 (-0.43%). The 8-sol rings agree as closely (Water and Ice exactly, Food -0.07%).

**Deviations and interpretations.**
- **EC7b's floor** is carved with `ExcavateFloor` (as `-garden` does) rather than `Debug_PaintCarve`; it is setup, before the snapshot.
- **R3's fixture needs crops live, and no crop row is live in the data** (all seven `SliceActive` FALSE), so in today's game no dig adds SpoilChemicals and that hook never fires. EC3 switches Potato on in memory for its one fixture (the Borer-test pattern) and switches it back.
- **EC3 covers the families an order or an event moves**: a Forge batch (Used 100 Regolith at start, Made 25 Struct at completion), the agent dig, the three haul-loss paths, a convoy to Zarya (Used 8 H2 and 1 SpareParts, Out 100 Struct; home, In 150 Ice), a ship landing, the shaft and floor spoil chemicals, a road and its refund, a furnished and sold plant, a Forge's salvage, and a growth project (nothing at its start; at completion Used Struct + the Lander's refund = the bill). Breathing, leaks, eating, greywater, the garden and production are pinned by EC4's conservation and EC7b. **Not fixtured**: the crisis drain, Big Arrival provisions, the discovery reward, robot repair (needs live Mass agents), covert loot (the detection roll is seeded by time of day) and launder. Their hooks are one line each and are covered only by review. The growth fixture's project placed every bunk, so `Credited` was 0 there: the nonzero refund path is not exercised.
- **EC8's "10-sol baseline colony"** is read as the suite's busy colony (EC7b's plus a Forge and a dig): pins for the battery's own baseline run would mean new lines in `baseline.log`, which must not move. EC8 pins 15 (slot, leg) lifetime totals in each band.
- **Version lines** moved in D6's suites too (`-caverns` CV8, `-deepdistricts` DD9, `-trams` TM6, `-depth` DP11, `-transit` TR18), as R4 asked. F16's message literal "(expect 50)" became "(expect 51)".
- **The convoy's fuel and wear** stay config scalars in the cost map (8 and 1, whole kilograms); only the parsed lot is rounded. The split is recorded nominal and gram-rounded (Observation 1).

**Tests.** New `-economy` suite, 71 checks, in `rh_battery.sh` (now 54 suites and 55 runs): EC0 data (21 rows >= 0, CSV = DT, the 18 placeholders, Hope / Research / Power valued 0 with no slot, 18 slots ascending); EC1 the inert twin in both bands (a full-precision digest of stocks, shelves, deposits, piles, air, batches, bank, Hope, quota, people and tasks, and the two saves byte-equal up to the E1 block, R7); EC2 rounding; EC3 above; EC4 conservation over 10 sols of the busy colony in each band, within 1 g + 0.05 g a load; EC5 the roll (the residual, a 12-sol jump, 1,000 rolls, one roll at the boundary in each band); EC6 the save (layout, save-load-save byte-identical, v50 refused, an unknown name skipped, an unsorted block, a negative ring entry and a bad head each reset with the colony loaded); EC7; EC8. **Mutations**, each built, run and restored by hash: dropping the breathing record fails 6 checks (first EC4); dropping `DigDeposit`'s record fails 14 (first EC7a).

**Battery.** `rh_battery.sh 10`: 0 suites not green; both pins hold. Against `battery_post_d6d`: 46 differing lines and the new `economy.log`. They are the save-version lines (50 to 51, R4's table plus D6's suites), every save file 5,997 bytes longer (the E1 block: DP11's and CV8's sizes, DC9's 3,718 to 9,715 bytes) and EP4's wall-clock digits. Nothing else moved.

**-scale** (outside the battery). Every digest is unchanged (N=5000 `5f9d7352`, recalled `b193ca3a`, reloaded `d6c87c14`). The ledger's timing is run last, on the N=5,000 city saved after its ladder line and loaded back, on and off in ABBA order with whole-sol era blocks: the agent step 0.3054 ms on / 0.3045 off (+0.0009 ms, 0.3%), the era step 0.3122 / 0.3097 (+0.0025 ms, 0.8%), 1,000 rolls 0.048 ms (budget 2). A first try, timed at the end of the run on D6c's colony, measured nothing useful: that colony loses its air and is evacuated within three sols, and the autosaves fell in the timed steps; it was replaced. Of two isolated runs, the first missed D6d's tram cold-commute budget (10.45 ms against 10, code E1 does not touch); the second was clean, 0 errors (9.88 ms). The run straight after the battery also missed D6c's and D6d's cold-commute budgets and the StepPower budget, as D6d's did.

**Playtest.** `RH_PLAYTEST_DISTRICTS=1 bash scripts/sim/rh_playtest.sh 110 e1_full guide full` is byte-identical to `Saved/RHPlaytest/d6d_full.txt` (1,468 lines, 0 sim errors).

**Click test.** Five runs at 1440x860: 72 / 72, 49 / 72, 72 / 72, 72 / 72, 66 / 72. E1 changes no RedHope code. The two failing runs failed on different first steps, each a click that never arrived (the section's open in one, the build placement in the other), and everything after it cascaded. This is recorded as flakiness under load, not fixed.

**Checkpoints.** `rh_checkpoints.sh all --force` re-made Midgame (sol 28.35) and Late (sol 62.35) at v51, 0 sim errors.

**Known gaps.** The six unfixtured families above; SpoilChemicals never made in live data; the click test's two flaky runs; D6d's tram `-scale` budget still has well under 1 ms of headroom.

### E2a manifest verbs: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-23, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-23/e2-earth-credit-design.md` (DIRECTOR ANSWER Q-A (a), Q-B (a); REVISION R1-R5), the first of the two commits REVISION observation 9 asks for: **the verbs only.** No money, no save bump, no stack cap.

**What E2a builds.**
- `DT_ManifestItems` gains `Verb` (FName), `Target` (FName) and `Amount` (**double**, so the Toolkit's 0.15 is HEAD's literal bit for bit), after `Effect`, which stays display text. CSV and DT synced; the CSV's other cells are byte-for-byte as before.
- `BuildManifestEffects` resolves every row once at world start into `ManifestEffects` (`ERHManifestVerb`, target, amount): `Stock` needs a resource row, `Kit` a building row, `Robot` and `RoverAboard` a robot row, `OrderLag` a `DT_Config` row, `FabSpeed` no target, `Crew` the style key `Pod` or `Transport`. A row that does not resolve logs a Warning and lands as nothing.
- `ApplyManifestItemEffect` keeps its signature and dispatches on the verb. Every branch is HEAD's statement for its rows: the same calls in the same order on the same keys (`ImportStock.FindOrAdd` at the same moment, so its saved key order is HEAD's), and the crew blocks' Gate-D-approved texts verbatim. E1's In records collapse to two sites, the `Stock` verb and the crew food (REVISION section 3.2).
- Read-only accessors for the suite: `FindManifestEffect`, `GetManifestEffectWarnings`, `GetFabricatorSpeedMul`, `GetFleetCount`, `GetImportStockOrder`.

**Deviations.** `Kit`, `Robot` and `RoverAboard` repeat their statement `round(Amount)` times; every row has 1, so this is HEAD's single statement. The `-credit` suite holds only CR0 and CR1 in E2a; CR2-CR10 come with E2b.

**Tests.** New `-credit` suite, 18 checks, in `rh_battery.sh` (55 suites, 56 runs): CR0 the data (14 rows resolve, 0 Warnings, each verb, target and amount HEAD's literal bit for bit, a Crew target is Pod or Transport, the CSV equals the DT); CR1 one fixture per row against HEAD's deltas (five stock rows and their E1 In record, kits and the rover with ImportStock's key order, both robots with one spawn broadcast each, the Toolkit's speed once and four times bit for bit, the Compute Core's lag, both crew blocks with no beds, with HEAD's alerts word for word, and with beds, a name with no row). **Mutations**, restored by hash: a `float` cast of the amount fails 2 (the Toolkit); dropping the Stock verb's In record fails 10.

**Battery.** 0 suites not green; both pins hold. Against `battery_post_e1`: 6 lines, all wall-clock digits (`economy` EC7a, `eraparity` EP4, `traffic` X16), and the new `credit.log`. Every `cargo unloaded`, crew and CEO line is unchanged.

**Playtest.** The 110-sol districts run is byte-identical to `e1_full`. **Click test** 72 / 72 twice (no RedHope code changed).

**-scale.** Every digest is identical (N=5000 `5f9d7352`, recalled `b193ca3a`, reloaded `d6c87c14`); the 80 Colonist Transports go through the `Crew` verb (arrivals 12-17 ms against 50). Three runs, one straight after the battery and two isolated, each missed wall-clock budgets in code E2a does not touch (the cold commutes, the cold deep net, StepPower, and in one run the approach points and GetColonyHope), by 5-30%. The machine was loaded throughout (load average 7-9: an iOS Simulator and a CrashReportClient that had been spinning since 10:03, left alone). Not re-tuned; recorded as environment.

### E2b Earth credit: what it settles, and where it deviated (2026-10-23, not yet committed)

Built from `docs/qa/2026-10-23/e2-earth-credit-design.md`: the DIRECTOR ANSWER (**Q-A (a):** each ship carries up to 30,000 kg; **Q-B (a):** the stack cap covers only effects that multiply or replace a value), REVISION R1-R5, the PROFESSOR REVIEW, the design. The second of the two E2 commits.

**What E2b builds.**
- **The balance.** `CreditBalanceMcr`, `CreditAwardedMcr`, `CreditSpentMcr` (int64 milli-credits, saved, never reset): Balance == Awarded - Spent, Balance >= 0. `CreditPay` is the only writer of income and the awarded total (E3 pays its sales through it); `PayQuotaAward(Kg)` = `CreditPay(floor(Kg x AwardMcrPerKg))`, called once in `StepQuota`'s met branch after the funding add, so the floor is taken once on the whole award. `LaunchShip` is the only debit and the only writer of the spent total. `AwardMassKg`, the CEO line and `OnQuotaMet` are untouched.
- **The cap (R2).** `ShipMassCapKg` is stamped from config at the transition to `AwaitingManifest` (before any payment) and cleared at landing; `AdvanceQuotaLadder` never touches it, so the knob-0 rest state has a cap of 0 while `AwardMassKg` stays stamped as HEAD left it.
- **The manifest.** `CanAddManifestItem` (const) runs the checks in order: the phase, the row (HEAD's two texts), the stack cap, the ship's mass cap, the credits. `AddManifestItem` and the deck's ADD both read it. `GetManifestCostMcr` sums `Price_mcr` in order. `LaunchShip` re-checks the cost against the balance before the phase changes (a live re-price refuses the launch; nothing is logged as an Error).
- **The stack cap.** `MaxStack` (Toolkit 4, Compute Core 1): landed plus aboard, refused at the purchase; `ManifestLanded` counts every landing of a capped row, the harness's deliveries included.
- **Data.** `RH_ManifestItems` gains `Price_mcr` (1,000 x Mass_kg) and `MaxStack`; `RH_Config` gains `AwardMcrPerKg` 1000 and `ShipMassCapKg` 30000; CSV and DTs synced; placeholders.
- **The bot (R3).** `LoadCargo` and its fill loop pre-check each item only where HEAD would have reached its budget check (the manifest open, the row live) and log HEAD's message byte for byte; the default run therefore spends exactly the award and leaves the carry-over. `carryover=1` (`RH_PLAYTEST_CARRYOVER=1`) drops the pre-check, and with it the rover room test measures against min(cap, balance) (observation 5).
- **Presentation.** The picker's two bars (Credits, Ship mass), a price on each row, ADD greyed by `CanAddManifestItem` with the reason as its tooltip, an "Earth credit" readout under the objective; `RH.Status` prints the manifest against the cap and the credit.
- **Save v52.** A credit block after E1's ledger block, the last thing in the file (72 bytes today): the three totals, the cap, then the capped rows by name with their landings. It repairs with Warnings (R5): negatives to 0, then Spent = Awarded - Balance, then Awarded = Balance if Spent would go negative; a cap of 0 in Open and Completed, the config value for an invalid cap with a ship open, a valid in-flight cap kept; an unsorted row list resets the counts.
- **The E1 suite learns the block after its own** (section 6): EC1 compares up to E1's block and checks the credit block is the same; EC6 finds E1's block before the credit block.

**A bug found by the gate and fixed.** E1's `ReadLedgerBlock` stopped reading at the first out-of-order name or negative ring entry. While E1's block was last in the file that was harmless; with the credit block after it, `ReadCreditBlock` read on from the middle of the ledger, and the R5 repair turned those bytes into a balance of about 6 x 10^18 mcr. Now a malformed ledger block is read to its end whenever its slot count is sane (each slot is a fixed size), and when the count itself is bad or the block is cut short the credit block is not read at all (`bLedgerBlockLost`): the credit starts at 0 with a Warning. EC6 now checks the credit after every doctored ledger and has a slot-count case; restoring the early stop fails 4 checks.

**Deviations.** CR3 appends through a sixth harness hook, `Debug_PushManifestItem` (no check, no log line), so its 240,000 decisions do not log a quarter of a million lines; its draws exclude the two capped rows (Lemma 1's hypothesis: the Toolkit is bought once).
